Machine: the official one, with 24 hardware threads, 8 fast P cores and 16 slower E cores, without hyperthreading
Premise: ingestion is bound by the crawlers' CPU, not by Mongo. In the preliminary run the crawlers used 400-790 % CPU and Mongo about 100 %, and JFR put over half of the crawler samples in GutenbergText.bookText
Series replicas: 1, 2 and 3 crawler replicas with 8 workers each
H1: throughput grows with replicas but less than linearly: 8 workers fit in the 8 P cores, while 16 and 24 spill onto the slower E cores, so the gain from 2 to 3 replicas is smaller than from 1 to 2
Series workers: 4, 8 and 16 workers in 1 crawler replica
H2: throughput grows from 4 to 8 workers, which still fit in the P cores, and flattens towards 16, whose extra workers run on the E cores
Time windows: the whole one, from the first download claim to the last book downloaded, is the main figure; the steady one, between the downloads that complete 10 % and 90 % of the books, shows how much start-up and tail weigh
[main] INFO org.ulpgc.tarantino.bench.corpus.CorpusTool - The corpus matches workload\corpus.sha256: 2800 books and 755974 distinct terms in benchmarks\runs\20261102-204917-ingest-throughput-77ed00a\corpus.json
